Try left camera first\n    me_file = find_versioned_file(sess_path, 'leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy')\n    times_file = find_versioned_file(sess_path, '_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy')\n    \n    if me_file is None or times_file is None:\n        # Try right camera\n        me_file = find_versioned_file(sess_path, 'rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy')\n        times_file = find_versioned_file(sess_path, '_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy')\n    \n    if me_file is None or times_file is None:\n        return None, None\n    \n    me_values = np.load(me_file).flatten()\n    me_times = np.load(times_file).flatten()\n    \n    # Ensure same length\n    min_len = min(len(me_values), len(me_times))\n    me_values = me_values[:min_len]\n    me_times = me_times[:min_len]\n    \n    # Remove NaN values\n    valid = ~np.isnan(me_values) & ~np.isnan(me_times)\n    me_values = me_values[valid]\n    me_times = me_times[valid]\n    \n    return me_times, me_values\n\n\ndef compute_trial_number_in_block(prob_left):\n    \"\"\"Compute trial number within each block.\n    \n    A block is a sequence of consecutive trials with the same probabilityLeft.\n    Trial number starts at 1 for the first trial in each block.\n    \"\"\"\n    trial_nums = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\n    current_block = prob_left.iloc[0] if hasattr(prob_left, 'iloc') else prob_left[0]\n    count = 0\n    \n    for i in range(len(prob_left)):\n        val = prob_left.iloc[i] if hasattr(prob_left, 'iloc') else prob_left[i]\n        if val == current_block:\n            count += 1\n        else:\n            current_block = val\n            count = 1\n        trial_nums[i] = count\n    \n    return trial_nums\n\n\ndef discretize_to_bins(values, n_bins=3, quantiles=None):\n    \"\"\"Discretize continuous values into n_bins categories using quantile boundaries.\n    \n    If quantiles are provided, use them. Otherwise compute from data.\n    Returns discretized values and the quantile boundaries used.\n    \"\"\"\n    if quantiles is None:\n        # Compute quantile boundaries\n        flat_vals = values[~np.isnan(values)].flatten()\n        quantiles = np.quantile(flat_vals, np.linspace(0, 1, n_bins + 1))\n        # Make boundaries slightly wider to include all values\n        quantiles[0] = -np.inf\n        quantiles[-1] = np.inf\n    \n    # Discretize\n    discretized = np.digitize(values, quantiles[1:-1])  # Returns 0, 1, ..., n_bins-1\n    discretized = np.clip(discretized, 0, n_bins - 1)\n    \n    return discretized.astype(np.float32), quantiles\n\n\ndef process_session(eid, probes_info, show_processing=False):\n    \"\"\"Process a single session.\n    \n    Returns: dict with session data, or None if session fails.\n    \"\"\"\n    t0 = time.time()\n    \n    # Get session path from first probe\n    first_probe = probes_info[0]\n    sess_path = find_session_path(first_probe['lab'], first_probe['subject'], first_probe['date'])\n    if sess_path is None:\n        print(f'  Session {eid}: no session path found')\n        return None\n    \n    # Load trials and mask\n    try:\n        trials_df, trials_mask = load_trials_and_mask(sess_path)\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f'  Session {eid}: failed to load trials: {e}')\n        return None\n    \n    # Load and merge spike data from all probes\n    probe_data = []\n    for probe_info in probes_info:\n        probe_name = probe_info['probe_name']\n        result = load_spike_data(sess_path, probe_name)\n        if result[0] is not None:\n            probe_data.append(result)\n        else:\n            print(f'  Session {eid}: failed to load probe {probe_name}')\n    \n    if not probe_data:\n        print(f'  Session {eid}: no probe data loaded')\n        return None\n    \n    # Merge probes\n    if len(probe_data) == 1:\n        spike_times, spike_clusters, n_clusters, cluster_brain_ids = probe_data[0]\n    else:\n        all_times = []\n        all_clusters = []\n        all_brain_ids = []\n        cluster_offset = 0\n        for st, sc, nc, cbi in probe_data:\n            all_times.append(st)\n            all_clusters.append(sc + cluster_offset)\n            all_brain_ids.append(cbi)\n            cluster_offset += nc\n        spike_times = np.concatenate(all_times)\n        spike_clusters = np.concatenate(all_clusters)\n        cluster_brain_ids = np.concatenate(all_brain_ids)\n        n_clusters = cluster_offset\n        # Sort by time\n        sort_idx = np.argsort(spike_times, kind='stable')\n        spike_times = spike_times[sort_idx]\n        spike_clusters = spike_clusters[sort_idx]\n    \n    # Map brain regions using Beryl\n    br = BrainRegions()\n    cluster_acronyms_raw = br.id2acronym(cluster_brain_ids)\n    cluster_acronyms_beryl = br.acronym2acronym(cluster_acronyms_raw, mapping='Beryl')\n    \n    # Apply trial mask\n    valid_trials = trials_df[trials_mask].copy()\n    \n    if len(valid_trials) < 2:\n        print(f'  Session {eid}: too few valid trials ({len(valid_trials)})')\n        return None\n    \n    # Compute intervals aligned to stimOn_times\n    stim_on = valid_trials[ALIGN_TIME].values\n    interval_starts = stim_on + TIME_WINDOW[0]\n    interval_ends = stim_on + TIME_WINDOW[1]\n    \n    # Bin spikes\n    t1 = time.time()\n    binned_spikes = bin_spikes_fast(\n        spike_times, spike_clusters, interval_starts, interval_ends,\n        n_clusters, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n    )\n    t_spike = time.time() - t1\n    \n    # Load and interpolate wheel speed\n    t1 = time.time()\n    wheel_times, wheel_speed = compute_wheel_speed(sess_path)\n    if wheel_times is None:\n        print(f'  Session {eid}: no wheel data')\n        return None\n    \n    wheel_binned, wheel_valid = interpolate_behavior(\n        wheel_times, wheel_speed, interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n    )\n    t_wheel = time.time() - t1\n    \n    # Load and interpolate whisker motion energy\n    t1 = time.time()\n    me_times, me_values = load_whisker_motion_energy(sess_path)\n    if me_times is None:\n        print(f'  Session {eid}: no whisker ME data')\n        return None\n    \n    me_binned, me_valid = interpolate_behavior(\n        me_times, me_values, interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n    )\n    t_me = time.time() - t1\n    \n    # Combined validity mask (all data must be valid)\n    combined_valid = wheel_valid & me_valid\n    \n    if np.sum(combined_valid) < 2:\n        print(f'  Session {eid}: too few valid trials after behavior filtering ({np.sum(combined_valid)})')\n        return None\n    \n    # Apply validity mask\n    binned_spikes = binned_spikes[combined_valid]\n    wheel_binned = wheel_binned[combined_valid]\n    me_binned = me_binned[combined_valid]\n    valid_trials_final = valid_trials[combined_valid].copy()\n    valid_trials_final.reset_index(drop=True, inplace=True)\n    \n    n_trials_final = len(valid_trials_final)\n    \n    # ---- Construct inputs ----\n    # Input 0: Time since stimulus onset (continuous, time-varying)\n    # Matches the bin centers used in reference code\n    time_since_stim = np.linspace(\n        TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE, TIME_WINDOW[1], N_BINS\n    ).astype(np.float32)\n    \n    # Input 1: Trial number in block (continuous, per-trial)\n    trial_num_in_block = compute_trial_number_in_block(\n        valid_trials_final['probabilityLeft']\n    )\n    \n    # ---- Construct outputs ----\n    # Output 0: Choice (binary, per-trial) left(-1)->0, right(1)->1\n    choice = valid_trials_final['choice'].values.copy()\n    choice_binary = np.where(choice == -1, 0, 1).astype(np.float32)\n    \n    # Output 1: Prior probability of left (per-trial) 0.2->0, 0.5->1, 0.8->2\n    prob_left = valid_trials_final['probabilityLeft'].values.copy()\n    prior_cat = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\n    prior_cat[prob_left == 0.2] = 0\n    prior_cat[prob_left == 0.5] = 1\n    prior_cat[prob_left == 0.8] = 2\n    \n    # Output 2 & 3: Wheel speed and Whisker ME (discretized, time-varying)\n    # Will be discretized globally after collecting all sessions\n    \n    t_total = time.time() - t0\n    print(f'  Session {eid}: {n_trials_final} trials, {n_clusters} neurons, '\n          f'{len(np.unique(cluster_acronyms_beryl))} regions '\n          f'(spike:{t_spike:.1f}s, wheel:{t_wheel:.1f}s, ME:{t_me:.1f}s, total:{t_total:.1f}s)')\n    \n    result = {\n        'eid': eid,\n        'subject': first_probe['subject'],\n        'lab': first_probe['lab'],\n        'binned_spikes': binned_spikes,  # (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)\n        'wheel_binned': wheel_binned,  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n        'me_binned': me_binned,  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n        'time_since_stim': time_since_stim,  # (n_bins,)\n        'trial_num_in_block': trial_num_in_block,  # (n_trials,)\n        'choice_binary': choice_binary,  # (n_trials,)\n        'prior_cat': prior_cat,  # (n_trials,)\n        'cluster_acronyms_beryl': cluster_acronyms_beryl,  # (n_clusters,)\n        'n_trials': n_trials_final,\n        'n_clusters': n_clusters,\n    }\n    \n    if show_processing:\n        result['trials_df'] = valid_trials_final\n        result['stim_on'] = stim_on[combined_valid]\n    \n    return result\n\n\ndef plot_processing(session_data, session_idx, save_prefix='processing'):\n    \"\"\"Plot processing steps for visual verification.\"\"\"\n    eid = session_data['eid']\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(4, 3, figsize=(20, 16))\n    fig.suptitle(f'Processing: {eid}', fontsize=14)\n    \n    # Plot 1: Neural activity raster for first trial\n    ax = axes[0, 0]\n    trial_data = session_data['binned_spikes'][0]  # (n_clusters, n_bins)\n    active = np.where(trial_data.sum(axis=1) > 0)[0]\n    if len(active) > 50:\n        active = active[:50]\n    if len(active) > 0:\n        ax.imshow(trial_data[active], aspect='auto', cmap='hot',\n                  extent=[TIME_WINDOW[0], TIME_WINDOW[1], len(active), 0])\n    ax.set_xlabel('Time from stim onset (s)')\n    ax.set_ylabel('Neuron')\n    ax.set_title('Trial 1: Neural activity')\n    ax.axvline(0, color='white', linestyle='--', alpha=0.7)\n    \n    # Plot 2: Mean neural activity across trials\n    ax = axes[0, 1]\n    mean_activity = session_data['binned_spikes'].mean(axis=0)  # (n_clusters, n_bins)\n    ax.plot(session_data['time_since_stim'], mean_activity.mean(axis=0))\n    ax.set_xlabel('Time from stim onset (s)')\n    ax.set_ylabel('Mean spike count')\n    ax.set_title('Mean neural activity')\n    ax.axvline(0, color='red', linestyle='--', alpha=0.7)\n    \n    # Plot 3: Wheel speed for first 5 trials\n    ax = axes[0, 2]\n    for i in range(min(5, session_data['n_trials'])):\n        ax.plot(session_data['time_since_stim'], session_data['wheel_binned'][i], alpha=0.5)\n    ax.set_xlabel('Time from stim onset (s)')\n    ax.set_ylabel('Wheel speed')\n    ax.set_title('Wheel speed (first 5 trials)')\n    ax.axvline(0, color='red', linestyle='--', alpha=0.7)\n    \n    # Plot 4: Whisker ME for first 5 trials\n    ax = axes[1, 0]\n    for i in range(min(5, session_data['n_trials'])):\n        ax.plot(session_data['time_since_stim'], session_data['me_binned'][i], alpha=0.5)\n    ax.set_xlabel('Time from stim onset (s)')\n    ax.set_ylabel('Whisker ME')\n    ax.set_title('Whisker ME (first 5 trials)')\n    ax.axvline(0, color='red', linestyle='--', alpha=0.7)\n    \n    # Plot 5: Choice distribution\n    ax = axes[1, 1]\n    choices, counts = np.unique(session_data['choice_binary'], return_counts=True)\n    ax.bar(['Left (0)', 'Right (1)'], [counts[choices==0][0] if 0 in choices else 0,\n                                        counts[choices==1][0] if 1 in choices else 0])\n    ax.set_title('Choice distribution')\n    \n    # Plot 6: Prior distribution\n    ax = axes[1, 2]\n    priors, counts = np.unique(session_data['prior_cat'], return_counts=True)\n    labels = ['0.2 (0)', '0.5 (1)', '0.8 (2)']\n    ax.bar([labels[int(p)] for p in priors], counts)\n    ax.set_title('Prior distribution')\n    \n    # Plot 7: Trial number in block\n    ax = axes[2, 0]\n    ax.plot(session_data['trial_num_in_block'])\n    ax.set_xlabel('Trial')\n    ax.set_ylabel('Trial # in block')\n    ax.set_title('Trial number in block')\n    \n    # Plot 8: Wheel speed histogram\n    ax = axes[2, 1]\n    ax.hist(session_data['wheel_binned'].flatten(), bins=50)\n    ax.set_xlabel('Wheel speed')\n    ax.set_title('Wheel speed distribution')\n    \n    # Plot 9: Whisker ME histogram\n    ax = axes[2, 2]\n    ax.hist(session_data['me_binned'].flatten(), bins=50)\n    ax.set_xlabel('Whisker ME')\n    ax.set_title('Whisker ME distribution')\n    \n    # Plot 10: Brain regions\n    ax = axes[3, 0]\n    regions, region_counts = np.unique(session_data['cluster_acronyms_beryl'], return_counts=True)\n    ax.barh(regions, region_counts)\n    ax.set_xlabel('# neurons')\n    ax.set_title('Brain regions')\n    \n    # Plot 11: Mean activity by choice\n    ax = axes[3, 1]\n    for c, label in [(0, 'Left'), (1, 'Right')]:\n        mask = session_data['choice_binary'] == c\n        if mask.sum() > 0:\n            mean_act = session_data['binned_spikes'][mask].mean(axis=(0, 1))\n            ax.plot(session_data['time_since_stim'], mean_act, label=label)\n    ax.legend()\n    ax.set_xlabel('Time from stim onset (s)')\n    ax.set_title('Mean activity by choice')\n    ax.axvline(0, color='red', linestyle='--', alpha=0.7)\n    \n    # Plot 12: Spike count per trial\n    ax = axes[3, 2]\n    spike_counts = session_data['binned_spikes'].sum(axis=(1, 2))\n    ax.plot(spike_counts)\n    ax.set_xlabel('Trial')\n    ax.set_ylabel('Total spikes')\n    ax.set_title('Spikes per trial')\n    \n    fig.tight_layout()\n    fig.savefig(f'{save_prefix}_{eid}.png', dpi=100)\n    plt.close(fig)\n    print(f'  Saved processing plot: {save_prefix}_{eid}.png')\n\n\ndef build_final_dataset(session_results):\n    \"\"\"Build the final dataset dictionary from processed session results.\"\"\"\n    \n    # First pass: collect all wheel speed and whisker ME values for global discretization\n    print('Computing global discretization boundaries...')\n    all_wheel = []\n    all_me = []\n    for sess in session_results:\n        all_wheel.append(sess['wheel_binned'].flatten())\n        all_me.append(sess['me_binned'].flatten())\n    \n    all_wheel = np.concatenate(all_wheel)\n    all_me = np.concatenate(all_me)\n    \n    # Remove NaN values\n    all_wheel = all_wheel[~np.isnan(all_wheel)]\n    all_me = all_me[~np.isnan(all_me)]\n    \n    # Compute quantile boundaries for 3 bins\n    wheel_quantiles = np.array([-np.inf, \n                                 np.quantile(all_wheel, 1/3), \n                                 np.quantile(all_wheel, 2/3), \n                                 np.inf])\n    me_quantiles = np.array([-np.inf,\n                              np.quantile(all_me, 1/3),\n                              np.quantile(all_me, 2/3),\n                              np.inf])\n    \n    print(f'  Wheel speed quantiles: {wheel_quantiles}')\n    print(f'  Whisker ME quantiles: {me_quantiles}')\n    \n    # Collect all unique subjects and brain regions\n    all_subjects = sorted(list(set(sess['subject'] for sess in session_results)))\n    subject_to_idx = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(all_subjects)}\n    \n    all_regions_set = set()\n    for sess in session_results:\n        all_regions_set.update(sess['cluster_acronyms_beryl'])\n    all_regions = sorted(list(all_regions_set))\n    region_to_idx = {r: i for i, r in enumerate(all_regions)}\n    \n    # Build the dataset\n    neural_list = []\n    input_list = []\n    output_list = []\n    subject_idx_list = []\n    brain_region_idx_list = []\n    \n    for sess in session_results:\n        n_trials = sess['n_trials']\n        n_clusters = sess['n_clusters']\n        \n        # Neural: list of (n_neurons, n_timepoints) arrays\n        session_neural = []\n        for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n            # binned_spikes is (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)\n            session_neural.append(sess['binned_spikes'][trial_idx].astype(np.float32))\n        neural_list.append(session_neural)\n        \n        # Input: (d_input, n_timepoints) or (d_input,) per trial\n        session_input = []\n        for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n            # input[0]: time since stim onset (time-varying) - same for all trials\n            # input[1]: trial number in block (per-trial) - broadcast to time-varying\n            inp = np.stack([\n                sess['time_since_stim'],  # (n_bins,)\n                np.full(N_BINS, sess['trial_num_in_block'][trial_idx], dtype=np.float32),  # (n_bins,)\n            ], axis=0)  # (2, n_bins)\n            session_input.append(inp)\n        input_list.append(session_input)\n        \n        # Output: (d_output, n_timepoints) or (d_output,) per trial\n        session_output = []\n        \n        # Discretize wheel speed and whisker ME for this session\n        wheel_disc = np.digitize(sess['wheel_binned'], wheel_quantiles[1:-1]).astype(np.float32)\n        wheel_disc = np.clip(wheel_disc, 0, 2)\n        me_disc = np.digitize(sess['me_binned'], me_quantiles[1:-1]).astype(np.float32)\n        me_disc = np.clip(me_disc, 0, 2)\n        \n        for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n            out = np.stack([\n                np.full(N_BINS, int(sess[\"choice_binary\"][trial_idx]), dtype=np.int64),  # (n_bins,)\n                np.full(N_BINS, int(sess[\"prior_cat\"][trial_idx]), dtype=np.int64),  # (n_bins,)\n                wheel_disc[trial_idx].astype(np.int64),  # (n_bins,)\n                me_disc[trial_idx].astype(np.int64),  # (n_bins,)\n            ], axis=0)  # (4, n_bins)\n            session_output.append(out)\n        output_list.append(session_output)\n        \n        # Subject index\n        subject_idx_list.append(subject_to_idx[sess['subject']])\n        \n        # Brain region index\n        region_idx = np.array([region_to_idx[r] for r in sess['cluster_acronyms_beryl']], dtype=np.int64)\n        brain_region_idx_list.append(region_idx)\n    \n    data = {\n        'neural': neural_list,\n        'input': input_list,\n        'output': output_list,\n        'subjects': all_subjects,\n        'subject_idx': np.array(subject_idx_list, dtype=np.int64),\n        'brain_regions': all_regions,\n        'brain_region_idx': brain_region_idx_list,\n        'input_names': ['time_since_stim_onset', 'trial_number_in_block'],\n        'output_names': ['choice', 'prior_probability_left', 'wheel_speed', 'whisker_motion_energy'],\n        'output_values': [\n            ['left', 'right'],  # choice: 0=left, 1=right\n            ['0.2', '0.5', '0.8'],  # prior: 0=0.2, 1=0.5, 2=0.8\n            ['low', 'medium', 'high'],  # wheel speed bins\n            ['low', 'medium', 'high'],  # whisker ME bins\n        ],\n        'metadata': {\n            'task_description': 'IBL Brain-wide Map: mouse performs visual detection task with biased blocks',\n            'time_bin_size': BINSIZE * 1000,  # in ms\n            'temporal_alignment_event': 'stimulus onset (stimOn_times)',\n            'off_start': TIME_WINDOW[0],  # -0.5s before stim onset\n            'off_end': TIME_WINDOW[1],  # 1.5s after stim onset\n            'binsize_s': BINSIZE,\n            'n_bins': N_BINS,\n            'align_time': ALIGN_TIME,\n            'time_window': TIME_WINDOW,\n            'trial_filtering': {\n                'min_rt': MIN_RT,\n                'max_rt': MAX_RT,\n                'max_trial_len': MAX_TRIAL_LEN,\n                'exclude_nochoice': EXCLUDE_NOCHOICE,\n                'nan_exclude': NAN_EXCLUDE,\n            },\n            'wheel_speed_quantiles': wheel_quantiles.tolist(),\n            'whisker_me_quantiles': me_quantiles.tolist(),\n            'source': 'IBL Brain-wide Map',\n            'reference_paper': 'IBL et al. 2023, A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour',\n            'methods_paper': 'Zhang et al. 2025, Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions',\n        },\n    }\n    \n    return data\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Convert IBL BWM data to decoder format')\n    parser.add_argument('output', type=str, help='Output pickle file path')\n    parser.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', default=True, help='Process all sessions (default)')\n    parser.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='Process only 2 sessions for testing')\n    parser.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true', help='Plot processing visualizations')\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n    \n    if args.sample:\n        args.full = False\n    \n    t_start = time.time()\n    \n    # Load session list\n    bwm_df = pd.read_csv(BWM_CSV, index_col=0)\n    print(f'BWM release: {len(bwm_df)} PIDs, {bwm_df.eid.nunique()} sessions, {bwm_df.subject.nunique()} subjects')\n    \n    # Group by session (eid)\n    sessions = {}\n    for _, row in bwm_df.iterrows():\n        eid = row.eid\n        if eid not in sessions:\n            sessions[eid] = []\n        sessions[eid].append({\n            'pid': row.pid,\n            'probe_name': row.probe_name,\n            'subject': row.subject,\n            'lab': row.lab,\n            'date': row.date,\n        })\n    \n    # Filter to available sessions\n    available_sessions = {}\n    for eid, probes in sessions.items():\n        sess_path = find_session_path(probes[0]['lab'], probes[0]['subject'], probes[0]['date'])\n        if sess_path is not None:\n            available_sessions[eid] = probes\n    \n    print(f'Available sessions: {len(available_sessions)}')\n    \n    if args.sample:\n        # Select 2 sessions for testing\n        eids = list(available_sessions.keys())[:2]\n        available_sessions = {eid: available_sessions[eid] for eid in eids}\n        print(f'Sample mode: processing {len(available_sessions)} sessions')\n    \n    # Process sessions\n    session_results = []\n    n_total = len(available_sessions)\n    n_failed = 0\n    \n    for i, (eid, probes) in enumerate(available_sessions.items()):\n        print(f'[{i+1}/{n_total}] Processing session {eid}...')\n        try:\n            result = process_session(eid, probes, show_processing=args.show_processing)\n            if result is not None:\n                if args.show_processing and len(session_results) < 2:\n                    plot_processing(result, len(session_results))\n                session_results.append(result)\n            else:\n                n_failed += 1\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f'  Session {eid}: FAILED with error: {e}')\n            import traceback\n            traceback.print_exc()\n            n_failed += 1\n    \n    print(f'\\nProcessed {len(session_results)} sessions successfully, {n_failed} failed')\n    \n    if len(session_results) == 0:\n        print('ERROR: No sessions processed successfully!')\n        sys.exit(1)\n    \n    # This metric quantifies motion in the whisker pad area and has a temporal resolution of the respective camera.\n     8→\n     9→We also performed markerless pose estimation of body parts using DLC31, which is used in a fully automated pipeline in IBL (v.2.1) to track various body parts s\n    10→uch as the paws, nose, tongue and pupil (Fig. 1d). In all analyses using DLC estimates, we excluded predictions with likelihood < 0.9. Furthermore, we developed\n    11→ several quality-control metrics for the DLC traces105.\n    12→\n    13→Overview of decoding\n    14→\n    15→We performed a decoding analysis to measure how much information the activity of populations of neurons contained about task variables such as stimulus side and\n    16→ choice. To do this we, used cross-validated, maximum-likelihood regression with L1 regularization (to zero out the contribution from noisy neurons). The neural\n    17→ regressors were defined by binning the spike counts from each neuron in each session in a given region in a specific time window on each trial. The duration of\n    18→ the time window, the number of bins in that time window (that is, the bin size) and the trial event to which it was aligned depended on the variable that is th\n    19→e target of our regression (Supplementary Table 2). These factors are discussed further below and include a variety of behavioural and task variables: stimulus\n    20→side, choice, feedback and wheel speed and velocity. Although a session may have included multiple probe insertions, we did not perform decoding on these probes\n    21→ separately because they are not independent. Instead, neurons in the same session and region were combined across probes for our decoding analysis. Decoding wa\n    22→s cross-validated and compared with a null distribution to test for significance. A given region may have been recorded on multiple sessions; therefore, in the\n    23→main figures (Figs. 4–7) the region P value was defined by combining session P values using Fisher’s combined probability test, and the region effect size was d\n    24→efined by subtracting the median of the null distribution from the decoding score and reporting the median of the resulting values across sessions. The P values\n    25→ for all regions were then subjected to FDR correction for multiple comparisons at q = 0.01.\n    26→\n    27→Decoding target variables\n    28→\n    29→Stimulus side, choice and feedback were treated as binary target variables for logistic regression. For stimulus side, trials that had zero contrast were exclud\n    30→ed. We used the LogisticRegression module from scikit-learn111 (v.1.1.2) with 0.001 tolerance, 20,000 maximum iterations, “l1” penalty, “liblinear” solver and “\n    31→fit_intercept” set to True. We balanced decoder classes by weighting samples by the inverse of the class frequency, 1/(2Pi,class). Decoding performance was eval\n    32→uated using the balanced accuracy of classification, which is the average of the recall probabilities for the two classes. Supplementary Figure 9 shows histogra\n    33→ms of the regression coefficients for all the variables.\n    34→\n    35→Wheel values (speed and velocity) change over the course of a trial, unlike the previous decoding targets, and we therefore had to treat these target variables\n    36→differently. We averaged wheel values in nonoverlapping 20-ms bins, starting 200 ms before first wheel-movement time and ending at 1,000 ms after first wheel-mo\n    37→vement time. Spike counts were similarly binned. The target value for a given bin (ending at time t) was decoded from spikes in a preceding (causal) window span\n    38→ning W bins (ending at times t, …, t-W). Therefore, if decoding from n neurons, there were (W + 1)n predictors of the target variable in a given bin. In practic\n    39→e, we used W = 10. To decode these continuous-valued targets, we performed linear regression using the Lasso module from scikit-learn111 (v.1.1.2) with 0.001 to\n    40→lerance, 1,000 maximum iterations and “fit_intercept” set to True. For each time-varying behavior (wheel-speed, whisker-motion-energy):\n    for beh in behaviors:\n        if beh == 'whisker-motion-energy':\n            target_dict = load_target_behavior(one, eid, 'left-whisker-motion-energy')\n            if 'skip' in target_dict.keys():\n                target_dict = load_target_behavior(one, eid, 'right-whisker-motion-energy')\n        else:\n            target_dict = load_target_behavior(one, eid, beh)\n        target_times, target_vals = target_dict['times'], target_dict['values']\n        target_times_list, target_vals_list, target_mask, skip_reasons = get_behavior_per_interval(\n            target_times, target_vals, intervals=intervals,\n            trials_df=trials_df, allow_nans=allow_nans, n_workers=n_workers, **kwargs\n        )\n        behave_dict.update({beh: np.array(target_vals_list, dtype=object)})\n        mask_dict.update({beh: target_mask})\n        behave_mask = np.logical_and(behave_mask, target_mask)\n    return behave_dict, mask_dict\n```\nNote: called with `beh_names[3:]` which is `['wheel-speed', 'whisker-motion-energy']`. Trial-level variables (choice, block, reward, contrast) are extracted from trials_df directly, not interpolated.\n\n### `get_behavior_per_interval`\n```python\ndef get_behavior_per_interval(target_times, target_vals, intervals=None, trials_df=None,\n                               allow_nans=True, n_workers=..., **kwargs):\n    binsize = kwargs['binsize']\n    align_interval = kwargs['time_window']\n    interval_len = align_interval[1] - align_interval[0]\n    if trials_df is not None:\n        align_event = kwargs['align_time']\n        align_times = trials_df[align_event].values\n        interval_begs = align_times + align_interval[0]\n        interval_ends = align_times + align_interval[1]\n    n_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / binsize))  # 100\n\n    # Split data into intervals\n    idxs_beg = np.searchsorted(target_times, interval_begs, side='right')\n    idxs_end = np.searchsorted(target_times, interval_ends, side='left')\n\n    def interpolate_behavior(target):\n        interval_idx, target_time, target_vals = target\n        # Skip conditions: empty data, NaN data, bad intervals, data starts too late, ends too early\n        if np.abs(interval_begs[interval_idx] - target_time[0]) > binsize:\n            skip_reason = 'target data starts too late'\n        if np.abs(interval_ends[interval_idx] - target_time[-1]) > binsize:\n            skip_reason = 'target data ends too early'\n        # Interpolation:\n        x_interp = np.linspace(interval_begs[interval_idx] + binsize, interval_ends[interval_idx], n_bins)\n        y_interp = interp1d(target_time, target_vals, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')(x_interp)\n        return interval_idx, is_good_interval, x_interp, y_interp, skip_reason\n```\nKey detail: interpolation x points are `np.linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)` -- i.e., shifted by one binsize from the start. This means the interpolated behavior values are at the **right edge** of each bin.\n\n### `align_spike_behavior`\n```python\ndef align_spike_behavior(binned_spikes, binned_behaviors, beh_names, trials_mask=None):\n    target_mask = [1] * len(binned_spikes)\n    for beh_name in beh_names:\n        beh_mask = [1 if trial is not None else 0 for trial in binned_behaviors[beh_name]]\n    target_mask = target_mask and beh_mask  # NOTE: Python `and` on lists returns last truthy value\n    if trials_mask is not None:\n        target_mask = target_mask and list(trials_mask.to_numpy().astype(int))\n    del_idxs = np.argwhere(np.array(target_mask) == 0)\n    aligned_binned_spikes = np.delete(binned_spikes, del_idxs, axis=0)\n    aligned_binned_behaviors = {}\n    for beh_name in beh_names:\n        aligned_binned_behaviors[beh_name] = np.delete(binned_behaviors[beh_name], del_idxs, axis=0)\n        aligned_binned_behaviors[beh_name] = np.array(\n            [y for y in aligned_binned_behaviors[beh_name]], dtype=float\n        ).reshape((aligned_binned_spikes.shape[0], -1))\n    return aligned_binned_spikes, aligned_binned_behaviors\n```\n\n### `interpolate_position` (from `wheel.py`)\n```python\ndef interpolate_position(re_ts, re_pos, freq=1000, kind='linear', fill_gaps=None):\n    t = np.arange(re_ts[0], re_ts[-1], 1 / freq)  # Uniform 1000Hz sampling\n    if t[-1] > re_ts[-1]:\n        t = t[:-1]\n    yinterp = interpolate.interp1d(re_ts, re_pos, kind=kind)(t)\n    if fill_gaps:\n        gaps, = np.where(np.diff(re_ts) >= fill_gaps)\n        for i in gaps:\n            yinterp[(t >= re_ts[i]) & (t < re_ts[i + 1])] = re_pos[i]\n    return yinterp, t\n```\n\n### `velocity_filtered` (from `wheel.py`)\n```python\ndef velocity_filtered(pos, fs, corner_frequency=20, order=8):\n    sos = scipy.signal.butter(N=order, Wn=corner_frequency / fs * 2, btype='lowpass', output='sos')\n    vel = np.insert(np.diff(scipy.signal.sosfiltfilt(sos, pos)), 0, 0) * fs\n    acc = np.insert(np.diff(vel), 0, 0) * fs\n    return vel, acc\n```\n\n### `SessionLoader.load_wheel` (from `one.py`)\n```python\ndef load_wheel(self, fs=1000, corner_frequency=20, order=8, collection=None):\n    wheel_raw = self.one.load_object(self.eid, 'wheel', collection=collection)\n    self.wheel['position'], self.wheel['times'] = interpolate_position(\n        wheel_raw['timestamps'], wheel_raw['position'], freq=fs)\n    self.wheel['velocity'], self.wheel['acceleration'] = velocity_filtered(\n        self.wheel['position'], fs=fs, corner_frequency=corner_frequency, order=order)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. Data Loading, Filtering, Alignment, and Binning Summary\n\n**Data Loading**:\n- Sessions indexed via `bwm_release.csv` (699 PIDs, 459 sessions, 139 subjects)\n- For each session: load all probes via `SpikeSortingLoader`, merge via `merge_probes`\n- No QC filtering (`qc=None`) -- all clusters used\n- Brain regions mapped from Allen CCF acronyms to Beryl mapping via `BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(..., mapping='Beryl')`\n\n**Trial Filtering** (via `load_trials_and_mask`):\n- Reaction time: 0.08s <= (firstMovement_times - stimOn_times) <= 2.0s\n- Trial length: (feedback_times - goCue_times) <= 10.0s\n- Exclude NaN in: stimOn_times, choice, feedback_times, probabilityLeft, firstMovement_times, feedbackType\n- Exclude no-choice trials (choice == 0)\n- Additional: trials without valid wheel-speed or whisker-motion-energy data are excluded by `align_spike_behavior`\n\n**Temporal Alignment**:\n- Aligned to `stimOn_times`\n- Time window: (-0.5, 1.5) seconds relative to stimulus onset\n- Each trial interval: `[stimOn_times - 0.5, stimOn_times + 1.5]`\n\n**Spike Binning**:\n- `binsize = 0.02s` (20ms)\n- `n_bins = ceil(2.0/0.02) = 100` bins per trial\n- Uses `bincount2D` from iblutil for spike count histograms\n- Output shape per trial: `(n_clusters, n_bins)` = `(n_neurons, 100)`\n\n**Behavioral Binning**:\n- Time-varying behaviors (wheel-speed, whisker-ME) interpolated to `n_bins=100` points via `interp1d(kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')`\n- Interpolation x-points: `np.linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)` (right-edge of bins)\n- Wheel speed: `abs(velocity)` where velocity comes from Butterworth-filtered (corner=20Hz, order=8) differentiation of 1000Hz interpolated position\n- Trial-level variables extracted directly from trials_df: choice, probabilityLeft (block), reward = (rewardVolume > 1)\n\n---\n\n## 4. `bwm_release.csv` Structure\n\nColumns: `pid, eid, probe_name, session_number, date, subject, lab`\n- 700 lines (header + 699 data rows)\n- 459 unique sessions (eids), 139 unique subjects, 699 unique PIDs, 11 labs\n- Example row: `56f2a378-...,6713a4a7-...,probe00,1,2020-02-18,NYU-11,angelakilab`\n\n---\n\n## 5. Output shape: `(n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)`, then each `.T` gives `(n_bins, n_clusters)`.\n\n### `get_spike_data_per_interval` (inner function)\n```python\nn_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / binsize))  # ceil(2.0/0.02) = 100\n\ndef compute_spike_count(interval):\n    interval_idx, t_beg, t_end = interval\n    idxs_t = (times >= t_beg) & (times < t_end)\n    times_curr = times[idxs_t]\n    clust_curr = clusters[idxs_t]\n    if times_curr.shape[0] == 0:\n        binned_spikes_tmp = np.zeros((n_clusters_in_region, n_bins))\n        ...\n    else:\n        binned_spikes_tmp, t_idxs, cluster_idxs = bincount2D(\n            times_curr, clust_curr, xbin=binsize, xlim=[t_beg, t_end])\n        _, idxs_tmp, _ = np.intersect1d(cluster_ids, cluster_idxs, return_indices=True)\n    return binned_spikes_tmp[:, :n_bins], idxs_tmp, interval_idx\n\nbinned_spikes = np.zeros((n_intervals, n_clusters_in_region, n_bins))\n# multiprocessing pool fills binned_spikes\n```\n\n### `load_target_behavior`\n```python\ndef load_target_behavior(one, eid, target):\n    sess_loader = SessionLoader(one, eid)\n    if target == 'wheel-speed':\n        sess_loader.load_wheel()\n        beh_dict = {\n            'times': sess_loader.wheel['times'].to_numpy(),\n            'values': np.abs(sess_loader.wheel['velocity'].to_numpy())  # speed = abs(velocity)\n        }\n    elif target == 'left-whisker-motion-energy':\n        sess_loader.load_motion_energy(views=['left'])\n        beh_dict = {\n            'times': sess_loader.motion_energy['leftCamera']['times'].to_numpy(),\n            'values': sess_loader.motion_energy['leftCamera']['whiskerMotionEnergy'].to_numpy()\n        }\n    # ... similar for right-whisker, wheel-position, wheel-velocity, pupil, etc.\n```\n\n### `bin_behaviors`\n```python\ndef bin_behaviors(one, eid, behaviors, intervals=None, trials_only=False, trials_df=None,\n                  mask=None, allow_nans=True, n_workers=os.cpu_count(), **kwargs):\n    behave_dict, mask_dict = {}, {}\n    if trials_df is not None:\n        choice = trials_df['choice'].to_numpy()\n        block = trials_df['probabilityLeft'].to_numpy()\n        reward = (trials_df['rewardVolume'] > 1).astype(int).to_numpy()\n        contrast = np.c_[trials_df['contrastLeft'], trials_df['contrastRight']]\n        contrast = (-1 * np.nan_to_num(contrast, 0)).sum(1)\n        behave_dict.update({'choice': choice, 'block': block, 'reward': reward, 'contrast': contrast})\n    # For each time-varying behavior (wheel-speed, whisker-motion-energy):\n    for beh in behaviors:\n        if beh == 'whisker-motion-energy':\n            target_dict = load_target_behavior(one, eid, 'left-whisker-motion-energy')\n            if 'skip' in target_dict.keys():\n                target_dict = load_target_behavior(one, eid, 'right-whisker-motion-energy')\n        else:\n            target_dict = load_target_behavior(one, eid, beh)\n        target_times, target_vals = target_dict['times'], target_dict['values']\n        target_times_list, target_vals_list, target_mask, skip_reasons = get_behavior_per_interval(\n            target_times, target_vals, intervals=intervals,\n            trials_df=trials_df, allow_nans=allow_nans, n_workers=n_workers, **kwargs\n        )\n        behave_dict.update({beh: np.array(target_vals_list, dtype=object)})\n        mask_dict.update({beh: target_mask})\n        behave_mask = np.logical_and(behave_mask, target_mask)\n    return behave_dict, mask_dict\n```\nNote: called with `beh_names[3:]` which is `['wheel-speed', 'whisker-motion-energy']`. Trial-level variables (choice, block, reward, contrast) are extracted from trials_df directly, not interpolated.\n\n### `get_behavior_per_interval`\n```python\ndef get_behavior_per_interval(target_times, target_vals, intervals=None, trials_df=None,\n                               allow_nans=True, n_workers=..., **kwargs):\n    binsize = kwargs['binsize']\n    align_interval = kwargs['time_window']\n    interval_len = align_interval[1] - align_interval[0]\n    if trials_df is not None:\n        align_event = kwargs['align_time']\n        align_times = trials_df[align_event].values\n        interval_begs = align_times + align_interval[0]\n        interval_ends = align_times + align_interval[1]\n    n_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / binsize))  # 100\n\n    # Split data into intervals\n    idxs_beg = np.searchsorted(target_times, interval_begs, side='right')\n    idxs_end = np.searchsorted(target_times, interval_ends, side='left')\n\n    def interpolate_behavior(target):\n        interval_idx, target_time, target_vals = target\n        # Skip conditions: empty data, NaN data, bad intervals, data starts too late, ends too early\n        if np.abs(interval_begs[interval_idx] - target_time[0]) > binsize:\n            skip_reason = 'target data starts too late'\n        if np.abs(interval_ends[interval_idx] - target_time[-1]) > binsize:\n            skip_reason = 'target data ends too early'\n        # Interpolation:\n        x_interp = np.linspace(interval_begs[interval_idx] + binsize, interval_ends[interval_idx], n_bins)\n        y_interp = interp1d(target_time, target_vals, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')(x_interp)\n        return interval_idx, is_good_interval, x_interp, y_interp, skip_reason\n```\nKey detail: interpolation x points are `np.linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)` -- i.e., shifted by one binsize from the start. This means the interpolated behavior values are at the **right edge** of each bin.\n\n### `align_spike_behavior`\n```python\ndef align_spike_behavior(binned_spikes, binned_behaviors, beh_names, trials_mask=None):\n    target_mask = [1] * len(binned_spikes)\n    for beh_name in beh_names:\n        beh_mask = [1 if trial is not None else 0 for trial in binned_behaviors[beh_name]]\n    target_mask = target_mask and beh_mask  # NOTE: Python `and` on lists returns last truthy value\n    if trials_mask is not None:\n        target_mask = target_mask and list(trials_mask.to_numpy().astype(int))\n    del_idxs = np.argwhere(np.array(target_mask) == 0)\n    aligned_binned_spikes = np.delete(binned_spikes, del_idxs, axis=0)\n    aligned_binned_behaviors = {}\n    for beh_name in beh_names:\n        aligned_binned_behaviors[beh_name] = np.delete(binned_behaviors[beh_name], del_idxs, axis=0)\n        aligned_binned_behaviors[beh_name] = np.array(\n            [y for y in aligned_binned_behaviors[beh_name]], dtype=float\n        ).reshape((aligned_binned_spikes.shape[0], -1))\n    return aligned_binned_spikes, aligned_binned_behaviors\n```\n\n### `interpolate_position` (from `wheel.py`)\n```python\ndef interpolate_position(re_ts, re_pos, freq=1000, kind='linear', fill_gaps=None):\n    t = np.arange(re_ts[0], re_ts[-1], 1 / freq)  # Uniform 1000Hz sampling\n    if t[-1] > re_ts[-1]:\n        t = t[:-1]\n    yinterp = interpolate.interp1d(re_ts, re_pos, kind=kind)(t)\n    if fill_gaps:\n        gaps, = np.where(np.diff(re_ts) >= fill_gaps)\n        for i in gaps:\n            yinterp[(t >= re_ts[i]) & (t < re_ts[i + 1])] = re_pos[i]\n    return yinterp, t\n```\n\n### `velocity_filtered` (from `wheel.py`)\n```python\ndef velocity_filtered(pos, fs, corner_frequency=20, order=8):\n    sos = scipy.signal.butter(N=order, Wn=corner_frequency / fs * 2, btype='lowpass', output='sos')\n    vel = np.insert(np.diff(scipy.signal.sosfiltfilt(sos, pos)), 0, 0) * fs\n    acc = np.insert(np.diff(vel), 0, 0) * fs\n    return vel, acc\n```\n\n### `SessionLoader.load_wheel` (from `one.py`)\n```python\ndef load_wheel(self, fs=1000, corner_frequency=20, order=8, collection=None):\n    wheel_raw = self.one.load_object(self.eid, 'wheel', collection=collection)\n    self.wheel['position'], self.wheel['times'] = interpolate_position(\n        wheel_raw['timestamps'], wheel_raw['position'], freq=fs)\n    self.wheel['velocity'], self.wheel['acceleration'] = velocity_filtered(\n        self.wheel['position'], fs=fs, corner_frequency=corner_frequency, order=order)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. Data Loading, Filtering, Alignment, and Binning Summary\n\n**Data Loading**:\n- Sessions indexed via `bwm_release.csv` (699 PIDs, 459 sessions, 139 subjects)\n- For each session: load all probes via `SpikeSortingLoader`, merge via `merge_probes`\n- No QC filtering (`qc=None`) -- all clusters used\n- Brain regions mapped from Allen CCF acronyms to Beryl mapping via `BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(..., mapping='Beryl')`\n\n**Trial Filtering** (via `load_trials_and_mask`):\n- Reaction time: 0.08s <= (firstMovement_times - stimOn_times) <= 2.0s\n- Trial length: (feedback_times - goCue_times) <= 10.0s\n- Exclude NaN in: stimOn_times, choice, feedback_times, probabilityLeft, firstMovement_times, feedbackType\n- Exclude no-choice trials (choice == 0)\n- Additional: trials without valid wheel-speed or whisker-motion-energy data are excluded by `align_spike_behavior`\n\n**Temporal Alignment**:\n- Aligned to `stimOn_times`\n- Time window: (-0.5, 1.5) seconds relative to stimulus onset\n- Each trial interval: `[stimOn_times - 0.5, stimOn_times + 1.5]`\n\n**Spike Binning**:\n- `binsize = 0.02s` (20ms)\n- `n_bins = ceil(2.0/0.02) = 100` bins per trial\n- Uses `bincount2D` from iblutil for spike count histograms\n- Output shape per trial: `(n_clusters, n_bins)` = `(n_neurons, 100)`\n\n**Behavioral Binning**:\n- Time-varying behaviors (wheel-speed, whisker-ME) interpolated to `n_bins=100` points via `interp1d(kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')`\n- Interpolation x-points: `np.linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)` (right-edge of bins)\n- Wheel speed: `abs(velocity)` where velocity comes from Butterworth-filtered (corner=20Hz, order=8) differentiation of 1000Hz interpolated position\n- Trial-level variables extracted directly from trials_df: choice, probabilityLeft (block), reward = (rewardVolume > 1)\n\n---\n\n## 4. `bwm_release.csv` Structure\n\nColumns: `pid, eid, probe_name, session_number, date, subject, lab`\n- 700 lines (header + 699 data rows)\n- 459 unique sessions (eids), 139 unique subjects, 699 unique PIDs, 11 labs\n- Example row: `56f2a378-...,6713a4a7-...,probe00,1,2020-02-18,NYU-11,angelakilab`\n\n---\n\n## 5. Contents of `methods.txt`\n\n72 lines. The text visible in the trajectory (terminal screen from episode-19) contained excerpts from the data paper covering:\n\n- **Video/camera setup**: Three cameras (left, right, body). Left/right at full resolution 150 Hz, body at half res 30 Hz\n- **Whisker motion energy**: Mean across pixels of absolute difference between adjacent frames, in whisker pad bounding box anchored by nose tip and eye (DLC)\n- **DLC pose estimation**: Excluded predictions with likelihood < 0.9\n- **Decoding overview**: Cross-validated max-likelihood regression with L1 regularization. Neural regressors = binned spike counts per neuron per trial. Neurons in same session and region combined across probes.\n- **Decoding targets**: Stimulus side, choice, feedback (binary, logistic regression); wheel speed/velocity (continuous, Lasso)\n- **Wheel binning**: 20ms nonoverlapping bins, starting 200ms before first wheel-movement, ending 1000ms after. The algorithm iterates through filtered trials and counts consecutive trials with the same `probabilityLeft` value. The counter resets to 1 whenever `probabilityLeft` changes. The result is a per-trial integer starting at 1 for the first trial in each block. This value is broadcast to all time bins for each trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_trial_number_in_block(prob_left):\n    trial_nums = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\n    current_block = prob_left.iloc[0] if hasattr(prob_left, 'iloc') else prob_left[0]\n    count = 0\n    for i in range(len(prob_left)):\n        val = prob_left.iloc[i] if hasattr(prob_left, 'iloc') else prob_left[i]\n        if val == current_block:\n            count += 1\n        else:\n            current_block = val\n            count = 1\n        trial_nums[i] = count\n    return trial_nums\n```\n\niii. The AI computes trial number in block on the **filtered** trials (after quality mask applied), not on the full trials table. This means if a trial is filtered out within a block, the trial count may not accurately reflect the true trial position within the original block. These are the standard IBL wheel data files, matching what `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` loads in the reference code.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Wheel speed*?\n\ni. Wheel position is: (1) interpolated to uniform 1000Hz sampling, (2) low-pass Butterworth filtered (corner=20Hz, order=8), (3) differentiated to get velocity, (4) absolute value taken to get speed. The speed is then interpolated to match neural time bins using `interp1d(kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')` at bin right edges.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Step 1: Interpolate to 1000Hz\nt_uniform = np.arange(timestamps[0], timestamps[-1], 1.0 / fs)\npos_interp = interp1d(timestamps, position, kind='linear')(t_uniform)\n# Step 2: Butterworth filter + velocity\nsos = scipy.signal.butter(N=order, Wn=corner_frequency / fs * 2, btype='lowpass', output='sos')\nvel = np.insert(np.diff(scipy.signal.sosfiltfilt(sos, pos_interp)), 0, 0) * fs\n# Step 3: Speed = abs(velocity)\nspeed = np.abs(vel).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. All wheel speed values across all trials and time bins are pooled, then 1/3 and 2/3 quantiles are computed. Values are binned using `np.digitize`.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Global quantiles:\nwheel_quantiles = np.array([-np.inf,\n                             np.quantile(all_wheel, 1/3),\n                             np.quantile(all_wheel, 2/3),\n                             np.inf])\n# Per session:\nwheel_disc = np.digitize(sess['wheel_binned'], wheel_quantiles[1:-1]).astype(np.float32)\nwheel_disc = np.clip(wheel_disc, 0, 2)\n```\n\niii. The algorithm iterates through filtered trials and counts consecutive trials with the same `probabilityLeft` value. The counter resets to 1 whenever `probabilityLeft` changes. The result is a per-trial integer starting at 1 for the first trial in each block. This value is broadcast to all time bins for each trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_trial_number_in_block(prob_left):\n    trial_nums = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\n    current_block = prob_left.iloc[0] if hasattr(prob_left, 'iloc') else prob_left[0]\n    count = 0\n    for i in range(len(prob_left)):\n        val = prob_left.iloc[i] if hasattr(prob_left, 'iloc') else prob_left[i]\n        if val == current_block:\n            count += 1\n        else:\n            current_block = val\n            count = 1\n        trial_nums[i] = count\n    return trial_nums\n```\n\niii. The AI computes trial number in block on the **filtered** trials (after quality mask applied), not on the full trials table. This means if a trial is filtered out within a block, the trial count may not accurately reflect the true trial position within the original block. The mapping follows the instruction specification: left = 0, right = 1. No-choice trials (choice == 0) are excluded by the trial filter.\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Choice*?\n\ni. The IBL choice values (-1 for left, 1 for right) are remapped to binary (0 for left, 1 for right). The value is broadcast to all time bins in the output array, making it a per-trial constant repeated across time.\n\nii.\n```python\nchoice_binary = np.where(choice == -1, 0, 1).astype(np.float32)\n# In output construction:\nnp.full(N_BINS, int(sess[\"choice_binary\"][trial_idx]), dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. This matches the instructions: \"Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\".\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Prior probability of left* derived from?\n\ni. Prior probability of left is derived from the `probabilityLeft` column in the trials table.\n\nii.\n```python\nprob_left = valid_trials_final['probabilityLeft'].values.copy()\nprior_cat = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\nprior_cat[prob_left == 0.2] = 0\nprior_cat[prob_left == 0.5] = 1\nprior_cat[prob_left == 0.8] = 2\n```\n\niii. This matches the instructions: \"Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\".\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Prior probability of left*?\n\ni. The continuous `probabilityLeft` values (0.2, 0.5, 0.8) are mapped to categorical values (0, 1, 2). The result is broadcast to all time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nprior_cat[prob_left == 0.2] = 0\nprior_cat[prob_left == 0.5] = 1\nprior_cat[prob_left == 0.8] = 2\n# In output:\nnp.full(N_BINS, int(sess[\"prior_cat\"][trial_idx]), dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The mapping is straightforward and matches the instructions exactly.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Wheel speed* derived from?\n\ni. Wheel speed is derived from `_ibl_wheel.position.npy` (wheel position) and `_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy` (wheel timestamps) in each session's alf directory.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition = np.load(pos_file).flatten()\ntimestamps = np.load(ts_file).flatten()\n```\n\niii. These are the standard IBL wheel data files, matching what `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` loads in the reference code.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Wheel speed*?\n\ni. Wheel position is: (1) interpolated to uniform 1000Hz sampling, (2) low-pass Butterworth filtered (corner=20Hz, order=8), (3) differentiated to get velocity, (4) absolute value taken to get speed. The speed is then interpolated to match neural time bins using `interp1d(kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')` at bin right edges.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Step 1: Interpolate to 1000Hz\nt_uniform = np.arange(timestamps[0], timestamps[-1], 1.0 / fs)\npos_interp = interp1d(timestamps, position, kind='linear')(t_uniform)\n# Step 2: Butterworth filter + velocity\nsos = scipy.signal.butter(N=order, Wn=corner_frequency / fs * 2, btype='lowpass', output='sos')\nvel = np.insert(np.diff(scipy.signal.sosfiltfilt(sos, pos_interp)), 0, 0) * fs\n# Step 3: Speed = abs(velocity)\nspeed = np.abs(vel).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. This matches the reference code's `interpolate_position` + `velocity_filtered` pipeline, and the `load_target_behavior` function which takes `abs()` of velocity for wheel-speed.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Wheel speed* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Wheel speed is discretized into 3 categories using global quantile boundaries (terciles computed across all sessions). All wheel speed values across all trials and time bins are pooled, then 1/3 and 2/3 quantiles are computed. Values are binned using `np.digitize`.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Global quantiles:\nwheel_quantiles = np.array([-np.inf,\n                             np.quantile(all_wheel, 1/3),\n                             np.quantile(all_wheel, 2/3),\n                             np.inf])\n# Per session:\nwheel_disc = np.digitize(sess['wheel_binned'], wheel_quantiles[1:-1]).astype(np.float32)\nwheel_disc = np.clip(wheel_disc, 0, 2)\n```\n\niii. The instructions say \"Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\" but don't specify the discretization method. Using quantile-based terciles ensures roughly equal distribution across bins.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Wheel speed* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Wheel speed is interpolated to the same time bins as neural data using `interp1d` with x-points `np.linspace(t_start + binsize, t_end, n_bins)`, matching the reference code's `get_behavior_per_interval`.\n\nii.\n```python\nx_interp = np.linspace(t_start + binsize, t_end, n_bins)\nf_interp = interp1d(seg_times, seg_vals, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')\nresult[trial_idx] = f_interp(x_interp)\n```\n\niii. Whisker motion energy is discretized into 3 categories using global quantile boundaries (terciles), the same approach as wheel speed.\n\nii.\n```python\nme_quantiles = np.array([-np.inf,\n                          np.quantile(all_me, 1/3),\n                          np.quantile(all_me, 2/3),\n                          np.inf])\nme_disc = np.digitize(sess['me_binned'], me_quantiles[1:-1]).astype(np.float32)\nme_disc = np.clip(me_disc, 0, 2)\n```\n\niii.
